       \beta_1\left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}-\widetilde A^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}
       +4\left\vert\beta-\theta\right\vert
       +\frac{2\beta_1\left\vert\beta-\theta\right\vert+\beta_1^2\left\vertt-s\right\vert}
                    {1-\beta_1^2}\right),
\end{align*}
where the last inequality substitutes the two iterate bounds just proved.
Enlarging the constant $C$ gives the residual bound in part~\textup{(ii)}.

For part~\textup{(iii)}, the Hessian is
$\nabla_u^2F_{\mathrm{TAP},\beta}
=\operatorname{diag}\left((1-u_i^2)^{-1}\right)
-\beta A^{\mathrm{full}}+\beta^2(1-q_\beta(t))I_n$.
{At the same $u$, the diagonal terms $\operatorname{diag}((1-u_i^2)^{-1})$ cancel each other, thus}
\begin{align*}
 &~\nabla_u^2F_{\mathrm{TAP},\beta}
      \left(u;A^{\mathrm{full}},y,q_\beta(t)\right)
   -\nabla_u^2F_{\mathrm{TAP},\theta}
      \left(u;\widetilde A^{\mathrm{full}},\widetilde y,q_\theta(s)\right)\\
 =&~-\beta A^{\mathrm{full}}+\theta\widetilde A^{\mathrm{full}}
    +(\beta^2(1-q_\beta(t))-\theta^2(1-q_\theta(s)))I_n.
\end{align*}
The operator norm of a scalar multiple of $I_n$ is the absolute value
of that scalar.  Hence
\begin{align*}
 &~\left\|\nabla_u^2F_{\mathrm{TAP},\beta}
      \left(u;A^{\mathrm{full}},y,q_\beta(t)\right)
   -\nabla_u^2F_{\mathrm{TAP},\theta}
      \left(u;\widetilde A^{\mathrm{full}},\widetilde y,q_\theta(s)\right)
   \right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\\
 \leq&~\left\Vert\beta A^{\mathrm{full}}
                 -\theta\widetilde A^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}
       +\left\vert\beta^2(1-q_\beta(t))-\theta^2(1-q_\theta(s))\right\vert\\
 \leq&~\beta_1
       \left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}-\widetilde A^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}
       +\left(4+\frac{2\beta_1}{1-\beta_1^2}\right)\left\vert\beta-\theta\right\vert
       +\frac{\beta_1^2}{1-\beta_1^2}\left\vertt-s\right\vert.
\end{align*}
Taking $C$ at least as large as these three coefficients proves
part~\textup{(iii)}.
<!-- end proof-40 -->
<!-- lemma-38: body_md -->
\label{lem:inverse-poly-initial-geometry}
Fix $0<\beta_1<1$ and let $t_0 = (1-\beta_1)^2 (1 - \beta_1^2)/(4 \beta_1^2)$.
There are $c,C>0$, depending only on $\beta_1$,
such that, for every $0<\beta\leq\beta_1$, with
$\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}$-probability at least $1-C\mathrm{e}^{-cn}$,
$\left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq4$ and
\begin{equation*}
 \forall t\in[0,t_0],\ y\in\mathbb{R}^n,\ u\in(-1,1)^n, \quad \nabla_u^2F_{\mathrm{TAP}}
   (u;A^{\mathrm{full}},y,q_\beta(t))\succeq \frac{(1 - \beta_1)^2}{2} I_n.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-38 -->
<!-- proof-41: body_md -->
Set
$\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}=\min(1/2,(1-\beta_1)^2/(4\beta_1))$.
By \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-planted-norm-tail}, with probability at least $1-C\mathrm{e}^{-cn}$, we have  $\left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq2+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}$.
On this event, we have
\begin{align*}
 \nabla_u^2F_{\mathrm{TAP}}
 =&~\operatorname{diag}((1-u_i^2)^{-1})
       -\beta A^{\mathrm{full}}+\beta^2(1-q_\beta(t))I_n\\
 \succeq&~((1-\beta)^2-\beta\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}
                      -\beta^2q_\beta(t))I_n\\
 \succeq&~\left(\frac34(1-\beta_1)^2
             -\frac{\beta_1^2t}{1-\beta_1^2}\right)I_n.
\end{align*}
{For the last inequality, we used \Cref{lem:q-beta-regularity} and the definition of $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}$.}
{For $t\le t_0$, we have $\beta_1^2t/(1-\beta_1^2)\leq(1-\beta_1)^2/4$, so the Hessian is at least $(1-\beta_1)^2I_n/2$.}
{Also $\|A^{\mathrm{full}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le2+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\le5/2<4$, completing the proof.}
<!-- end proof-41 -->
<!-- lemma-39: body_md -->
\label{lem:inverse-poly-positive-geometry}
Fix $0<\beta_1<1$ and $s>0$. There are constants $r,\kappa>0$ and an integer $K_0\geq0$ such that, for every fixed $K\geq K_0$, there are constants $c,C>0$ and an open rectangle $U\subseteq(0,1)\times(s/2,2s)$ containing $(\beta_1,s)$, such that the following holds.


{For every $n$ and every $(\beta,t)\in U$, with $\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}$-probability at least $1-C\mathrm{e}^{-cn}$, the following hold simultaneously:}
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item $(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_t)$ satisfies $(\kappa r/16)$-small TAP
residual and $(4r,4\kappa)$-local strong convexity at inverse temperature
$\beta$, time $t$, and AMP depth $K$, as defined in
\Cref{def:amp-tap-geometry}.
\item
\begin{equation*}
  \left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq4,\qquad
  \left\VertY_t\right\Vert_2\leq C\sqrt n,\qquad
  \left\VertY_t\right\Vert_\infty\leq n^2.
\end{equation*}
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-39 -->
<!-- lemma-40: body_md -->
\label{lem:inverse-poly-conditional-good-event}
Fix $0<\beta_1<1$ and {$s>0$}.
There exist $r,\kappa>0$ and an integer $K_0\geq0$, such that, for every fixed $K\geq K_0$, sufficiently large $n$ and every
$x\in\set{\pm1}^n$, under the planted construction at inverse
temperature $\beta_1$, we have
\begin{equation*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\in E_x\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\in E_x\mid X=x\right]}\geq\frac12.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-40 -->
<!-- proof-42: body_md -->
We first show that $\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\notin E_X\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\notin E_X\right]}=o(1)$. We verify each constraint and use a union bound.
Apply \Cref{prop:fixed-time-amp-tap-input} at $(\beta_1,s)$ and
choose $r,\kappa>0$ small enough, set $\varepsilon=\kappa r/64$ and let $K_0$ be the depth
threshold in \Cref{prop:fixed-time-amp-tap-input}.
{Fix any $K\geq K_0$. With probability tending to one, we have}
\begin{equation*}
 \nabla_u^2F_{\mathrm{TAP},\beta_1}
   \left(u;A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s,q_{\beta_1}(s)\right)
 \succeq8\kappa I_n
 \quad\text{for every }u\in
 B\left(\widehat m_{K,\beta_1}(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s),8r\sqrt n\right)
       \cap(-1,1)^n,
\end{equation*}
and the TAP residual at this AMP center is at most
$\varepsilon\sqrt n\leq\kappa r\sqrt n/64$.
The matrix bound $\left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq3$ follows from \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-planted-norm-tail},
and the noise bound $\left\Verty-sx\right\Vert_2\leq2\sqrt{sn}$ follows from $Y_s-sX\sim N(0,sI_n)$.
A union bound therefore gives
$\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\notin E_X\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\notin E_X\right]}=o(1)$.

Next, we claim that for any $x, x'\in \set{\pm 1}^n$, we have 
\[
\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\in E_x\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\in E_x\mid X=x\right]} = \ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\in E_{x'}\mid X=x'\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\in E_{x'}\mid X=x'\right]}.
\]
Then, this lemma follows directly.

We now prove this claim. Fix $x,x'\in\set{\pm1}^n$.
Let $D=\operatorname{diag}(x_1x'_1,\ldots,x_nx'_n)$, so that
$D^\top=D$, $D^2=I_n$, and $Dx=x'$.
{We condition on $X=x$ and write $A^{\mathrm{full}}=W+\beta_1xx^\top/n$ and $Y_s=sx+\sqrt sG$, where $W\sim\mathrm{GOE}(n)$ and $G\sim N(0,I_n)$ are independent.}  Then
\begin{equation*}
 D^\top A^{\mathrm{full}}D=D^\top WD+\frac{\beta_1}{n}x'(x')^\top,
 \qquad DY_s=sx'+\sqrt sDG.
\end{equation*}
Since $(D^\top WD,DG)$ has the same joint law as $(W,G)$, we have
\begin{equation*}
 \mathcal L(D^\top A^{\mathrm{full}}D,DY_s\mid X=x)
 =\mathcal L(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s\mid X=x'),
\end{equation*}
where $\mathcal L$ denotes the distribution under the planted construction.
{It suffices to verify that, for every $(A^{\mathrm{full}},y)$,}
\begin{equation*}
 (A^{\mathrm{full}},y)\in E_x
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 (D^\top A^{\mathrm{full}}D,Dy)\in E_{x'}.
\end{equation*}

First, the matrix and noise bounds are preserved because
$\left\VertD^\top A^{\mathrm{full}}D\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}
=\left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}$ and
$\left\VertDy-sx'\right\Vert_2=\left\VertD(y-sx)\right\Vert_2=\left\Verty-sx\right\Vert_2$.
Next, the AMP recursion satisfies
\begin{equation*}
 \widehat m_{K,\beta_1}(D^\top A^{\mathrm{full}}D,Dy)
 =D\widehat m_{K,\beta_1}(A^{\mathrm{full}},y).
\end{equation*}
Indeed, its initial vectors are zero.  If its iterates at the transformed
input are $Dz_k,Dm_k,Dm_{k-1}$, the next update is
\begin{align*}
 \beta_1D^\top A^{\mathrm{full}}D(Dm_k)+Dy
       -\beta_1^2\left(1-\frac{\left\VertDm_k\right\Vert_2^2}{n}\right)Dm_{k-1}
 =&~ D\left(\beta_1A^{\mathrm{full}}m_k+y
       -\beta_1^2\left(1-\frac{\left\Vertm_k\right\Vert_2^2}{n}\right)m_{k-1}\right)\\
 =&~ Dz_{k+1}.
\end{align*}
Also $\tanh(Dz_{k+1})=D\tanh(z_{k+1})$, completing the induction.

For the residual, its definition and
$\operatorname{arctanh}(Du)=D\operatorname{arctanh}(u)$ give
\begin{align*}
 R_{\mathrm{TAP},\beta_1}(Du;D^\top A^{\mathrm{full}}D,Dy,q)
 =&~D\operatorname{arctanh}(u)-Dy
       -\beta_1D^\top A^{\mathrm{full}}D(Du)
       +\beta_1^2(1-q)Du\\
 =&~D R_{\mathrm{TAP},\beta_1}(u;A^{\mathrm{full}},y,q).
\end{align*}
Taking $u=\widehat m_{K,\beta_1}(A^{\mathrm{full}},y)$ and
$q=q_{\beta_1}(s)$ shows that the residual norm is unchanged.
Finally, writing the Hessian as
$\operatorname{diag}((1-u_i^2)^{-1})
-\beta_1A^{\mathrm{full}}+\beta_1^2(1-q)I_n$, we obtain
\begin{equation*}
 \nabla^2F_{\mathrm{TAP},\beta_1}
       (Du;D^\top A^{\mathrm{full}}D,Dy,q)
 =D^\top\bigl[\nabla^2F_{\mathrm{TAP},\beta_1}
       (u;A^{\mathrm{full}},y,q)\bigr]D.
\end{equation*}
The function $u\mapsto Du$ sends the ball around the original AMP center
onto the ball of the same radius around the transformed center,
preserves $(-1,1)^n$, and preserves the lower bound $8\kappa I_n$.
Thus every condition defining $E_x$ is equivalent to the corresponding
condition defining $E_{x'}$.
{Combining this equivalence with the preceding identity in distribution, {we obtain}}
\begin{align*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\in E_x\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\in E_x\mid X=x\right]}
 =&~\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[(D^\top A^{\mathrm{full}}D,DY_s)\in E_{x'}\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{}\left[(D^\top A^{\mathrm{full}}D,DY_s)\in E_{x'}\mid X=x\right]}\\
 =&~\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\in E_{x'}\mid X=x'\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\in E_{x'}\mid X=x'\right]}.
\end{align*}
These probabilities are therefore equal for all $x$ and equal to their
average over $X$.  Hence
\begin{equation*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\in E_x\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\in E_x\mid X=x\right]}
 =\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\in E_X\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\in E_X\right]}=1-o(1)\geq1/2
\end{equation*}
for all sufficiently large $n$, with a threshold independent of $x$.
<!-- end proof-42 -->
<!-- lemma-41: body_md -->
\label{lem:inverse-poly-anchor-geometry}
Take $r,\kappa,K_0$ from \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-conditional-good-event}
and fix $K\geq K_0$.
There exist $c,C>0$, depending only on $(\beta_1,s,K)$, such that for
every $n$, with probability at least $1-C\mathrm{e}^{-cn}$ under the planted
construction at inverse temperature $\beta_1$, the following hold
simultaneously:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item $(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)$ satisfies $(\kappa r/32)$-small TAP
residual at inverse temperature $\beta_1$, time $s$, and AMP depth $K$,
as defined in \Cref{def:amp-tap-geometry}.
\item $(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)$ satisfies $(7r,7\kappa)$-local strong
convexity at the same inverse temperature, time, and AMP depth.
\item $\left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq7/2$ and
$\left\VertY_s-sX\right\Vert_2\leq3\sqrt{sn}$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-41 -->
<!-- proof-43: body_md -->
Fix $x\in\set{\pm1}^n$.
By \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-conditional-good-event},
\begin{equation*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\in E_x\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{}\left[(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\in E_x\mid X=x\right]}\geq1/2
\end{equation*}
for all sufficiently large $n$.  {We condition on $X=x$.}
We claim that there is $\delta>0$, depending only on $(\beta_1,s,K)$,
such that whenever $(A_0^{\mathrm{full}},y_0)\in E_x$ and
\begin{equation*}
 \frac n2\left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}-A_0^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm F}^2
 +\frac1s\left\Verty-y_0\right\Vert_2^2\leq\delta^2n,
\end{equation*}
then the following holds.
Let 
$\widehat m=\widehat m_{K,\beta_1}(A^{\mathrm{full}},y)$ and
$\widehat m_0=\widehat m_{K,\beta_1}(A_0^{\mathrm{full}},y_0)$.
We have $\left\Vert\widehat m-\widehat m_0\right\Vert_2\leq r\sqrt n$ and 
\begin{align*}
 \left\VertR_{\mathrm{TAP},\beta_1}(\widehat m;A^{\mathrm{full}},y,q_{\beta_1}(s))
 -R_{\mathrm{TAP},\beta_1}(\widehat m_0;A_0^{\mathrm{full}},y_0,q_{\beta_1}(s))\right\Vert_2
 \leq \kappa r\sqrt n/64.
\end{align*}
For every $u\in(-1,1)^n$,
\begin{equation*}
 \left\Vert\nabla_u^2F_{\mathrm{TAP},\beta_1}(u;A^{\mathrm{full}},y,q_{\beta_1}(s))
 -\nabla_u^2F_{\mathrm{TAP},\beta_1}(u;A_0^{\mathrm{full}},y_0,q_{\beta_1}(s))\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}
 \leq\kappa.
\end{equation*}
Also $\left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq7/2$ and
$\left\Verty-sx\right\Vert_2\leq3\sqrt{sn}$.

{To prove the claim, let $C$ be the constant in \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-fixed-depth-stability} for depth $K$ and inverse temperatures at most $\beta_1$.
We choose $\delta>0$ so that}
\begin{equation*}
 \delta\leq\min\left(1,\frac1{2\sqrt2},
 \frac{r}{C(\sqrt2+\sqrt s)},
 \frac{\kappa r}{64C(\sqrt2+\sqrt s)},
 \frac{\kappa}{\sqrt2C}\right).
\end{equation*}
The assumed distance bound implies
$\left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}-A_0^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}
\leq\sqrt2\delta$ and $\left\Verty-y_0\right\Vert_2\leq\delta\sqrt{sn}$.
{Since $(A_0^{\mathrm{full}},y_0)\in E_x$, the triangle inequality gives}
\begin{equation*}
 \left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq3+\sqrt2\delta\leq7/2,
 \qquad \left\Verty-sx\right\Vert_2\leq(2+\delta)\sqrt{sn}\leq3\sqrt{sn}.
\end{equation*}
Both matrices therefore satisfy the operator-norm bound required by
\Cref{lem:inverse-poly-fixed-depth-stability}.
{We apply its part~\textup{(ii)} at the same inverse temperature $\beta_1$ and time $s$.}
Each of the center and residual differences is at most
$C(\sqrt2+\sqrt s)\delta\sqrt n$.
Our choice of $\delta$ bounds this quantity by both $r\sqrt n$ and
$\kappa r\sqrt n/64$.
Part~\textup{(iii)} bounds the Hessian difference at each fixed $u$ by
$\sqrt2C\delta\leq\kappa$, proving the claim.

{Consequently, whenever $(A_0^{\mathrm{full}},y_0)\in E_x$ and the preceding distance bound holds, $(A^{\mathrm{full}},y)$ also satisfies parts~\textup{(i)--(iii)} of this lemma.}
Indeed, the residual norm is at most
$\kappa r\sqrt n/64+\kappa r\sqrt n/64=\kappa r\sqrt n/32$.
For every $u\in B(\widehat m,7r\sqrt n)$,
$\left\Vertu-\widehat m_0\right\Vert_2\leq\left\Vertu-\widehat m\right\Vert_2
+\left\Vert\widehat m-\widehat m_0\right\Vert_2<8r\sqrt n$.
On this ball intersected with $(-1,1)^n$, the original Hessian is at least
$8\kappa I_n$, so the perturbed Hessian is at least $7\kappa I_n$.

\begingroup

It remains to apply \Cref{lem:gaussian-enlargement-boosting} to a distance function on the Gaussian input generating the planted model given $X=x$.
Write $N_G=n(n+1)/2+n$ and let $\xi=(g,h)\sim N(0,I_{N_G})$ be independent of $X$, where $g=(g_{ij})_{i\leq j}$ and $h=(h_i)_{i\leq n}$.
Define the symmetric matrix $A_x(\xi)$ and field $y_x(\xi)$ by
\begin{equation*}
 (A_x(\xi))_{ij}=\frac{\beta_1x_ix_j}{n}+\frac{g_{ij}}{\sqrt n} (i<j),
 \quad (A_x(\xi))_{ii}=\frac{\beta_1}{n}+\sqrt{\frac2n}g_{ii},
 \quad (y_x(\xi))_i=sx_i+\sqrt s\,h_i.
\end{equation*}
For every $x\in\set{\pm1}^n$, this pair has the law of $(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)$ given $X=x$.
Define
\begin{equation*}
 \mathcal G_x\triangleq
 \left\{\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{N_G}:(A_x(\zeta),y_x(\zeta))\in E_x\right\},
\end{equation*}
and define
\begin{equation*}
 \mathcal H_x\triangleq
 \left\{\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{N_G}:(A_x(\zeta),y_x(\zeta))
 \text{ satisfies parts~\textup{(i)--(iii)} of this lemma}\right\}.
\end{equation*}
These sets are Borel because the finite-depth AMP output, residual, and Hessian are continuous in the Gaussian input, and failure of the Hessian condition is an open condition in the data and the trial point.
By \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-conditional-good-event}, for all sufficiently large $n$ and every $x\in\set{\pm1}^n$,
$\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[\xi\in\mathcal G_x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{}\left[\xi\in\mathcal G_x\right]}\geq\frac12$.

For every $x\in\set{\pm1}^n$, define the $1$-Lipschitz function
$F_x(z)\triangleq\operatorname{dist}(z,\mathcal G_x)$ for any $z\in\mathbb{R}^{N_G}$.
Since $F_x\geq0$ and $\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[F_x(\xi)=0\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{}\left[F_x(\xi)=0\right]}\geq\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[\xi\in\mathcal G_x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{}\left[\xi\in\mathcal G_x\right]}\geq1/2$, zero is a median of $F_x(\xi)$.

We next verify the stability inclusion.
For $\xi=(g,h)$ and $\zeta=(g',h')$, the coordinate scalings give
\begin{align*}
 &~\frac n2\left\VertA_x(\xi)-A_x(\zeta)\right\Vert_{\mathrm F}^2
       +\frac1s\left\Verty_x(\xi)-y_x(\zeta)\right\Vert_2^2\\
 =&~\frac n2\left(\frac2n\sum_{i<j}(g_{ij}-g'_{ij})^2
                   +\frac2n\sum_i(g_{ii}-g'_{ii})^2\right)
       +\sum_i(h_i-h'_i)^2
 =\left\Vert\xi-\zeta\right\Vert_2^2.
\end{align*}
If $F_x(\xi)\leq\delta\sqrt n/2$, then some $\zeta\in\mathcal G_x$ satisfies $\left\Vert\xi-\zeta\right\Vert_2<\delta\sqrt n$.
The preceding identity and the perturbation claim show that $\xi\in\mathcal H_x$.
Thus, for every $x\in\set{\pm1}^n$,
$\left\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{N_G}:F_x(\xi)\leq\frac{\delta\sqrt n}{2}\right\}
 \subseteq\mathcal H_x$.
For each $x$, we apply \Cref{lem:gaussian-enlargement-boosting} to $F_x$ with $L=1$, $q=0$, and $u=\delta\sqrt n/2$, and then average this bound over $X$ to obtain
\begin{equation*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[\xi\notin\mathcal H_X\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{}\left[\xi\notin\mathcal H_X\right]}
 \leq\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[F_X(\xi)>\delta\sqrt n/2\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{}\left[F_X(\xi)>\delta\sqrt n/2\right]}
 \leq 2\Phi(-\delta\sqrt n/2)
 \leq 2\exp(-\delta^2n/8).
\end{equation*}
Since $(A_X(\xi),y_X(\xi))$ has the planted law at inverse temperature $\beta_1$ and time $s$, this is the required failure bound for parts~\textup{(i)--(iii)}.
Finally, increasing $C$ covers the finitely many smaller dimensions and finishes the proof.
\endgroup
<!-- end proof-43 -->
<!-- proof-44: body_md -->
{We take $r,\kappa,K_0$ from \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-conditional-good-event}, fix any $K\geq K_0$, and apply \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-anchor-geometry} at $(\beta_1,s)$.}
{It remains to transfer these bounds to nearby inverse temperatures and times through \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-fixed-depth-stability}.}
Draw $X\sim\operatorname{Unif}(\set{\pm1}^n)$,
$W\sim\mathrm{GOE}(n)$, and $G\sim N(0,I_n)$ independently.
For two points $(\beta_1,s)$ and $(\beta,t)$, we set
\begin{equation*}
 A_{\beta_1}^{\mathrm{full}}=W+\beta_1 XX^\top/n,\quad
 A_{\beta}^{\mathrm{full}}=W+\beta XX^\top/n,\quad
 Y_s=sX+\sqrt sG,\quad Y_t=tX+\sqrt tG.
\end{equation*}
On the event in
\Cref{lem:inverse-poly-anchor-geometry}, we have $\left\VertG\right\Vert_2\leq3\sqrt n$,
and for $t\in(s/2,2s)$,
\begin{equation*}
 \left\VertA_{\beta}^{\mathrm{full}}-A_{\beta_1}^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}
 =|\beta-\beta_1|,\qquad
 \left\VertY_t-Y_s\right\Vert_2
 \leq( |t-s|+3|\sqrt t-\sqrt s|)\sqrt n
 \leq C_s|t-s|\sqrt n.
\end{equation*}
We apply \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-fixed-depth-stability} on the temperature interval $[\beta_1/2,(1+\beta_1)/2]$, and choose $U\subseteq(\beta_1/2,(1+\beta_1)/2)\times(s/2,2s)$.
Parts~\textup{(ii)--(iii)} of
\Cref{lem:inverse-poly-fixed-depth-stability} bound the center and
residual changes by $C(|\beta-\beta_1|+|t-s|)\sqrt n$, and the
Hessian change by $C(|\beta-\beta_1|+|t-s|)$.
Choose an open rectangle $U$ around $(\beta_1,s)$ so small that these
changes are at most $r\sqrt n$, $\kappa r\sqrt n/32$, and $\kappa$,
respectively.  Also require $|\beta-\beta_1|\leq1/2$ throughout $U$.
{By \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-anchor-geometry}-\textup{(iii)} and $\|A_\beta^{\mathrm{full}}-A_{\beta_1}^{\mathrm{full}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=|\beta-\beta_1|$, we obtain $\|A_\beta^{\mathrm{full}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le7/2+1/2=4$.}

The $4r\sqrt n$ ball around the AMP center at $(\beta,t)$ lies in the
$7r\sqrt n$ ball around the AMP center at $(\beta_1,s)$.
{The Hessian at inverse temperature $\beta$ and time $t$ is at least $6\kappa I_n$}
throughout the smaller ball intersected with $(-1,1)^n$,
and its center residual is at most $\kappa r\sqrt n/16$.
Also $\left\VertY_t\right\Vert_2\leq(t+3\sqrt t)\sqrt n$ and
$\left\VertY_t\right\Vert_\infty\leq n^2$ for large $n$.
For each parameter pair in $U$, failure is contained in the complement
of the event in \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-anchor-geometry}, whose
probability is at most $C\mathrm{e}^{-cn}$.
<!-- end proof-44 -->
<!-- lemma-42: body_md -->
\label{lem:inverse-poly-positive-posterior}
Choose the fixed depth $K$ in \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-positive-geometry}
also above the threshold in \Cref{prop:fixed-time-amp-tap-input}
{at $(\beta_1,s)$ with $\varepsilon=r/(4\sqrt2)$.}
For these parameters, the rectangle $U$ in
\Cref{lem:inverse-poly-positive-geometry} can be chosen so that, for every
fixed integer $p\geq1$ and every $(\beta,t)\in U$, with
$\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}$-probability at least
$1-C_{\beta_1,s,p}n^{-p}$, the pair $(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_t)$ satisfies
$r$-posterior-mean proximity at inverse temperature $\beta$, time $t$,
and AMP depth $K$, as defined in \Cref{def:amp-tap-geometry}.
<!-- end lemma-42 -->
<!-- lemma-43: body_md -->
\label{lem:inverse-poly-fixed-depth-concentration}
Fix an integer $K\geq0$ and a compact rectangle {$I\subseteq(0,1)\times(0,\infty)$}.
{At each $(\beta,t)\in I$, let $\widehat m_K(Y_t)$ be the AMP center at inverse temperature $\beta$, and set}
\begin{equation*}
 S_2(\beta,t)=\frac{\left\Vert\widehat m_K(Y_t)\right\Vert_2^2}{n},\qquad
 S_3(\beta,t)=\frac{\langle X,\widehat m_K(Y_t)\rangle}{n}.
\end{equation*}
For $j\in\{2,3\}$ and every fixed integer $p\geq1$,
\begin{equation*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[
  \left|S_j(\beta,t)
   -\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_j(\beta,t)\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_j(\beta,t)\right]}\right|^{2p}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[
  \left|S_j(\beta,t)
   -\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_j(\beta,t)\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_j(\beta,t)\right]}\right|^{2p}\right]}
 \leq C_{I,K,p}n^{-p}.
\end{equation*}
For every $(\beta,t),(\beta_1,s)\in I$,
\begin{align*}
 \left|\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_j(\beta,t)\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_j(\beta,t)\right]}
       -\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta_1}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_j(\beta_1,s)\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta_1}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_j(\beta_1,s)\right]}\right|
 \leq C_{I,K}(|\beta-\beta_1|+|t-s|)+C_{I,K}\mathrm{e}^{-c_I n}.
\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-43 -->
<!-- proof-45: body_md -->
We first prove the concentration.
{We fix $(\beta,t)\in I$ and condition on $X=x$.}
Take independent standard Gaussian variables $(g_{ij})_{i\leq j}$ and
$(h_i)_{i\leq n}$, and write
\begin{equation*}
 A^{\mathrm{full}}_{ij}=\frac{\beta x_ix_j}{n}+\frac{g_{ij}}{\sqrt n}
 \ (i<j),\qquad
 A^{\mathrm{full}}_{ii}=\frac{\beta}{n}+\sqrt{\frac2n}g_{ii},
 \qquad Y_{t,i}=tx_i+\sqrt t\,h_i.
\end{equation*}
Collect these variables into $\xi=(g,h)$, where $g=(g_{ij})_{i\leq j}$
and $h=(h_i)_{i\leq n}$.
For two such standard Gaussian inputs $\xi,\widetilde\xi$, the scaling gives
\begin{equation*}
 \left\Vert\xi-\widetilde\xi\right\Vert_2^2
 =\frac n2\left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}-\widetilde A^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm F}^2
  +\frac1t\left\Verty-\widetilde y\right\Vert_2^2.
\end{equation*}
Consequently,
$\sqrt n\left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}-\widetilde A^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}
\leq\sqrt2\left\Vert\xi-\widetilde\xi\right\Vert_2$ and
$\left\Verty-\widetilde y\right\Vert_2\leq\sqrt t\left\Vert\xi-\widetilde\xi\right\Vert_2$.
When both matrix norms are at most $4$,
\Cref{lem:inverse-poly-fixed-depth-stability}-\textup{(ii)} bounds the
change of the AMP output by $C_{I,K}\left\Vert\xi-\widetilde\xi\right\Vert_2$.
Let $u,v$ be the AMP outputs corresponding to $\xi,\widetilde\xi$,
respectively.  Both belong to $[-1,1]^n$, so
$\left\Vertu+v\right\Vert_2\leq2\sqrt n$.
Writing $S_j(\xi)$ for the scalar evaluated at the data generated by
$\xi$, with $(\beta,t,x)$ fixed, we obtain
\begin{align*}
 |S_2(\xi)-S_2(\widetilde\xi)|
 =&~\frac{|\left\Vertu\right\Vert_2^2-\left\Vertv\right\Vert_2^2|}{n}
 =\frac{|\langle u+v,u-v\rangle|}{n}\\
 \leq&~\frac{\left\Vertu+v\right\Vert_2}{n}\left\Vertu-v\right\Vert_2
 \leq\frac{2}{\sqrt n}\left\Vertu-v\right\Vert_2
 \leq\frac{2C_{I,K}}{\sqrt n}\left\Vert\xi-\widetilde\xi\right\Vert_2.
\end{align*}
For $S_3$, the planted spin is the same fixed $x$ at both inputs and
$\left\Vertx\right\Vert_2=\sqrt n$.  Hence
\begin{align*}
 |S_3(\xi)-S_3(\widetilde\xi)|
 =\frac{|\langle x,u-v\rangle|}{n}
 \leq\frac{\left\Vertx\right\Vert_2}{n}\left\Vertu-v\right\Vert_2
 =\frac1{\sqrt n}\left\Vertu-v\right\Vert_2
 \leq\frac{C_{I,K}}{\sqrt n}\left\Vert\xi-\widetilde\xi\right\Vert_2.
\end{align*}
Absorbing the factor two into $C_{I,K}$, each $S_j$, $j\in\{2,3\}$,
is therefore $C_{I,K}/\sqrt n$-Lipschitz in the standard Gaussian input
on the set where the matrix norm is at most $4$.

\begingroup

{We have proved this Lipschitz property on the set $\left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}\le4$.
For each fixed $x$, we use McShane's extension theorem and clip the extension to $[-1,1]$, preserving its Lipschitz constant; denote the resulting function by $\overline S_{j,x}$, which agrees with $S_j$ on the matrix-norm cutoff.
Doing this for every $x$ defines the family $(\overline S_{j,x})_{x\in\set{\pm1}^n}$, and under the planted construction we set $\overline S_j=\overline S_{j,X}(\xi)$, where $X$ and $\xi$ are independent.}
{For each $x\in\set{\pm1}^n$, we apply the moment conclusion of \Cref{lem:gaussian-enlargement-boosting} to $\overline S_{j,x}$ with $L=C_{I,K}/\sqrt n$.}
Since $\xi$ is independent of $X$, for every integer $p\geq1$ and every $x\in\set{\pm1}^n$, we obtain
\begin{equation*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\overline S_j-\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\overline S_j\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[\overline S_j\mid X=x\right]}\right|^{2p}\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[\left|\overline S_j-\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\overline S_j\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[\overline S_j\mid X=x\right]}\right|^{2p}\mid X=x\right]}
 \leq C_{I,K,p}n^{-p}.
\end{equation*}
Next, we compare $S_j$ with its extension $\overline S_j$.
{Given $X=x$, we have $A^{\mathrm{full}}=W+\beta xx^\top/n$.
Conjugating by $\operatorname{diag}(x)$ shows that the distribution of $\|A^{\mathrm{full}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ does not depend on $x$.
Since the inverse temperatures in $I$ are bounded above by a constant smaller than one, the proof of \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-planted-norm-tail} gives constants $C_I,c_I>0$ such that}
\begin{align*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[\left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}>4\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{}\left[\left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}>4\mid X=x\right]}
 =\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[\left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}>4\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{}\left[\left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}>4\right]} \leq C_I\mathrm{e}^{-c_In}{.}
\end{align*}
Since $|S_j|$ and $|\overline S_j|$ are bounded by one,
\begin{equation*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[|S_j-\overline S_j|^{2p}\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[|S_j-\overline S_j|^{2p}\mid X=x\right]}\leq2^{2p}C_I\mathrm{e}^{-c_In}.
\end{equation*}
Jensen's inequality also gives
\begin{equation*}
 \left|\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\overline S_j-S_j\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[\overline S_j-S_j\mid X=x\right]}\right|^{2p}
 \leq\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[|\overline S_j-S_j|^{2p}\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[|\overline S_j-S_j|^{2p}\mid X=x\right]}
 \leq2^{2p}C_I\mathrm{e}^{-c_In}.
\end{equation*}
We decompose
\begin{equation*}
 S_j-\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_j\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[S_j\mid X=x\right]}
 =(S_j-\overline S_j)
 +(\overline S_j-\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\overline S_j\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[\overline S_j\mid X=x\right]})
 +\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\overline S_j-S_j\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[\overline S_j-S_j\mid X=x\right]}.
\end{equation*}
Using $|a+b+c|^{2p}\leq3^{2p-1}(|a|^{2p}+|b|^{2p}+|c|^{2p})$,
we obtain
\begin{align*}
 &~\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[|S_j-\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_j\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[S_j\mid X=x\right]}|^{2p}\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[|S_j-\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_j\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[S_j\mid X=x\right]}|^{2p}\mid X=x\right]}\\
 \leq&~3^{2p-1}\left(
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[|S_j-\overline S_j|^{2p}\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[|S_j-\overline S_j|^{2p}\mid X=x\right]}
 +\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[|\overline S_j-\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\overline S_j\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[\overline S_j\mid X=x\right]}|^{2p}\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[|\overline S_j-\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\overline S_j\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[\overline S_j\mid X=x\right]}|^{2p}\mid X=x\right]}
 +\left|\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\overline S_j-S_j\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[\overline S_j-S_j\mid X=x\right]}\right|^{2p}\right).
\end{align*}
Substituting the preceding bounds, we obtain
\begin{equation*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[|S_j-\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_j\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[S_j\mid X=x\right]}|^{2p}\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[|S_j-\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_j\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[S_j\mid X=x\right]}|^{2p}\mid X=x\right]}
 \leq3^{2p-1}\left(2^{2p+1}C_I\mathrm{e}^{-c_In}
 +C_{I,K,p}n^{-p}\right).
\end{equation*}
For each fixed $p$, $n^p\mathrm{e}^{-c_In}\leq(p/(\mathrm{e} c_I))^p$.
After increasing the constant depending only on $(I,K,p)$, we therefore obtain
\begin{equation*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[|S_j-\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_j\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[S_j\mid X=x\right]}|^{2p}\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[|S_j-\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_j\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[S_j\mid X=x\right]}|^{2p}\mid X=x\right]}
 \leq C_{I,K,p}n^{-p}.
\end{equation*}
\endgroup

By the same sign-change argument as in the proof of
\Cref{lem:inverse-poly-conditional-good-event}, the conditional law of
$S_j$ given $X=x$ does not depend on $x$: both the squared AMP norm and
its overlap with the planted spin are unchanged by the sign change.
Hence $\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_j\mid X=x\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{}\left[S_j\mid X=x\right]}=\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_j\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_j\right]}$.
Averaging the conditional moment bound proves the first part of this lemma.

Next, we prove the stability.
We couple two quantities $\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_j(\beta,t)\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_j(\beta,t)\right]}$ and $\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta_1}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_j(\beta_1,s)\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta_1}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_j(\beta_1,s)\right]}$ by using a common uniform spin $X$, full GOE matrix $W$, and independent $G\sim N(0,I_n)$.
Hence we have $A_\beta^{\mathrm{full}}=W+\beta XX^\mathsf T/n$,
$A_{\beta_1}^{\mathrm{full}}=W+\beta_1 XX^\mathsf T/n$,
$Y_t=tX+\sqrt tG$, and $Y_s=sX+\sqrt sG$.
Then
\begin{equation*}
 \left\VertA_\beta^{\mathrm{full}}-A_{\beta_1}^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}
 =|\beta-\beta_1|,\qquad
 Y_t-Y_s=(t-s)X+(\sqrt t-\sqrt s)G.
\end{equation*}
On the event
$\left\VertA_\beta^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq4$ and $\left\VertA_{\beta_1}^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq4$,
part~\textup{(ii)} of
\Cref{lem:inverse-poly-fixed-depth-stability} implies
\begin{equation*}
 |S_j(\beta,t)-S_j(\beta_1,s)|
 \leq C_{I,K}\left(|\beta-\beta_1|+|t-s|
       +|\sqrt t-\sqrt s|\frac{\left\VertG\right\Vert_2}{\sqrt n}\right).
\end{equation*}
The time coordinates in $I$ are bounded away from zero, so
$|\sqrt t-\sqrt s|\leq C_I|t-s|$, and
$\ifthenelse{\isempty{G\sim N(0,I_n)}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\VertG\right\Vert_2\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{G\sim N(0,I_n)}\left[\left\VertG\right\Vert_2\right]}\leq\sqrt n$.
The event
$\left\VertA_\beta^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}>4$ or
$\left\VertA_{\beta_1}^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}>4$
has probability at most $C_I\mathrm{e}^{-c_In}$, and
$|S_j(\beta,t)-S_j(\beta_1,s)|\leq2$.
Taking expectations gives the second part of this lemma.
<!-- end proof-45 -->
<!-- proof-46: body_md -->
Recall from \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-fixed-depth-concentration} that
$S_2(\beta,t)=\left\Vert\widehat m_K(Y_t)\right\Vert_2^2/n$ and
$S_3(\beta,t)=\langle X,\widehat m_K(Y_t)\rangle/n$.
Set $S_1(\beta,t)=\left\Vertm(A,Y_t)\right\Vert_2^2/n$.
Since $\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[X\mid A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_t\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[X\mid A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_t\right]}=m(A,Y_t)$, expanding the squared distance gives
\begin{equation}
  \frac{\left\Vertm\left(A,Y_t\right)-\widehat m_{K}\left(Y_t\right)\right\Vert_2^2}{n}
  =S_1(\beta,t)+S_2(\beta,t)-2\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\mid A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_t\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\mid A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_t\right]}.
  \label{eq:inverse-poly-three-scalars}
\end{equation}
We now prove the concentration of the above quantity.
For the first term, by \Cref{lem:tap-overlap-mgf}, we use $q_\beta(t)$ to approximate it.
For the other two terms, we use their expectations. 
This gives the next approximation
\begin{equation*}
 a_n(\beta,t)\triangleq q_\beta(t)+\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_2(\beta,t)\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_2(\beta,t)\right]}
                    -2\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\right]}.
\end{equation*}
Let $r$ be the radius constant from
\Cref{lem:inverse-poly-positive-geometry}.
We claim that $U$ can be chosen independently of $p$ so that, for every
fixed integer $p\geq1$ and every $(\beta,t)\in U$,
for all sufficiently large $n$, we have
\begin{equation*}
 a_n(\beta,t)\leq r^2/4,\qquad
  \ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\vert
    \frac{\left\Vertm\left(A,Y_t\right)-\widehat m_{K}\left(Y_t\right)\right\Vert_2^2}{n}
    -a_n(\beta,t)\right\vert^{2p}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[\left\vert
    \frac{\left\Vertm\left(A,Y_t\right)-\widehat m_{K}\left(Y_t\right)\right\Vert_2^2}{n}
    -a_n(\beta,t)\right\vert^{2p}\right]}\leq C_{\beta_1,s,p}n^{-p}.
\end{equation*}


Assume the claim.  If
$\left\Vertm(A,Y_t)-\widehat m_K(Y_t)\right\Vert_2>r\sqrt n$, then
\begin{equation*}
 \frac{\left\Vertm(A,Y_t)-\widehat m_K(Y_t)\right\Vert_2^2}{n}-a_n(\beta,t)
 >r^2-a_n(\beta,t)\geq\frac{3r^2}{4}.
\end{equation*}
Applying Markov's inequality to the $2p$-th power
of the absolute difference, we obtain
\begin{align*}
 &~\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}
 \left\{\left\Vertm(A,Y_t)-\widehat m_K(Y_t)\right\Vert_2>r\sqrt n\right\}\\
 \leq&~\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}\left\{
 \left|\frac{\left\Vertm(A,Y_t)-\widehat m_K(Y_t)\right\Vert_2^2}{n}
 -a_n(\beta,t)\right|>\frac{3r^2}{4}\right\}\\
 \leq&~\left(\frac4{3r^2}\right)^{2p}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|
 \frac{\left\Vertm(A,Y_t)-\widehat m_K(Y_t)\right\Vert_2^2}{n}
 -a_n(\beta,t)\right|^{2p}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[\left|
 \frac{\left\Vertm(A,Y_t)-\widehat m_K(Y_t)\right\Vert_2^2}{n}
 -a_n(\beta,t)\right|^{2p}\right]}
 \leq C_{\beta_1,s,p}n^{-p}.
\end{align*}
Increasing the constant to cover the finitely many smaller $n$ proves
the lemma.

{It remains to prove the claim.
We first bound $a_n(\beta_1,s)$ and then use the parameter stability estimates to bound $a_n(\beta,t)$ throughout $U$.}
\begingroup
By the choice of $K$ and \Cref{prop:fixed-time-amp-tap-input}-\textup{(ii)} at $(\beta_1,s)$ with $\varepsilon=r/(4\sqrt2)$, we have
\begin{equation*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta_1}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[
 \frac{\|m(A,Y_s)-\widehat m_{K,\beta_1}(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\|_2^2}{n}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta_1}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[
 \frac{\|m(A,Y_s)-\widehat m_{K,\beta_1}(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_s)\|_2^2}{n}\right]}
 \leq\frac{r^2}{32}+o(1).
\end{equation*}
\endgroup
Taking expectations in
\cref{eq:inverse-poly-three-scalars} under the planted construction
at inverse temperature $\beta_1$ yields
\begin{align*}
  a_n(\beta_1,s)
  =\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta_1}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{\left\Vertm\left(A,Y_s\right)-\widehat m_{K}\left(Y_s\right)\right\Vert_2^2}{n}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta_1}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[\frac{\left\Vertm\left(A,Y_s\right)-\widehat m_{K}\left(Y_s\right)\right\Vert_2^2}{n}\right]}
    +q_{\beta_1}\left(s\right)-\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta_1}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_1(\beta_1,s)\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta_1}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_1(\beta_1,s)\right]}.
\end{align*}
{For the second term, we condition on $(A,Y_t)$, draw $\sigma^1,\sigma^2$ independently from $\mu_{A,Y_t}$, and set $R_{12}=\langle \sigma^1,\sigma^2\rangle/n$.}
Then we have that $S_1(\beta,t)=\ifthenelse{\isempty{(\sigma^1,\sigma^2)\sim\mu_{A,Y_t}^{\otimes2}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[R_{12}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{(\sigma^1,\sigma^2)\sim\mu_{A,Y_t}^{\otimes2}}\left[R_{12}\right]}$.
{By \Cref{lem:tap-overlap-mgf} and Jensen's inequality, we have}
\begin{align*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[|S_1(\beta,t)-q_\beta(t)|^{2p}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[|S_1(\beta,t)-q_\beta(t)|^{2p}\right]}
 \leq&~\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\ifthenelse{\isempty{(\sigma^1,\sigma^2)\sim\mu_{A,Y_t}^{\otimes2}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[
 |R_{12}-q_\beta(t)|^{2p}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{(\sigma^1,\sigma^2)\sim\mu_{A,Y_t}^{\otimes2}}\left[
 |R_{12}-q_\beta(t)|^{2p}\right]}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[\ifthenelse{\isempty{(\sigma^1,\sigma^2)\sim\mu_{A,Y_t}^{\otimes2}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[
 |R_{12}-q_\beta(t)|^{2p}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{(\sigma^1,\sigma^2)\sim\mu_{A,Y_t}^{\otimes2}}\left[
 |R_{12}-q_\beta(t)|^{2p}\right]}\right]}
 \leq C_{\beta_1,s,p}n^{-p}.
\end{align*}
Taking $p=1$ and applying Cauchy--Schwarz at $(\beta_1,s)$ gives
\begin{equation*}
 |q_{\beta_1}(s)-\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta_1}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_1(\beta_1,s)\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta_1}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_1(\beta_1,s)\right]}|
 \leq\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta_1}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[|S_1(\beta_1,s)-q_{\beta_1}(s)|^2\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta_1}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[|S_1(\beta_1,s)-q_{\beta_1}(s)|^2\right]}^{1/2}
 \leq C_{\beta_1,s}n^{-1/2}.
\end{equation*}
{Combining the two estimates, we obtain $a_n(\beta_1,s)\leq r^2/32+o(1)+C_{\beta_1,s}n^{-1/2}\leq r^2/8$ for all sufficiently large $n$.}

Next, we extend this upper bound to any other $(\beta, t) \in U$.
Parts~\textup{(i)--(ii)} of \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-fixed-depth-stability}
and \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-fixed-depth-concentration} imply
\begin{equation*}
 a_n(\beta,t)\leq a_n(\beta_1,s)
       +C(|\beta-\beta_1|+|t-s|)+C\mathrm{e}^{-cn}.
\end{equation*}
Shrink $U$ around $(\beta_1,s)$, keeping its closure inside the original
rectangle, so that
$a_n(\beta,t)\leq r^2/4$ for $(\beta,t)\in U$ for all sufficiently large $n$.

We now prove the moment bound in the claim.  Subtracting the definition
of $a_n$ from \cref{eq:inverse-poly-three-scalars}, we have
\begin{align*}
 \frac{\left\Vertm(A,Y_t)-\widehat m_K(Y_t)\right\Vert_2^2}{n}-a_n(\beta,t)
 =&~S_1(\beta,t)-q_\beta(t)
    +S_2(\beta,t)-\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_2(\beta,t)\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_2(\beta,t)\right]}\\
 &~-2\left(\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\mid A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_t\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\mid A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_t\right]}
             -\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\right]}\right).
\end{align*}
The first term has the overlap moment bound just established.
For the second term, \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-fixed-depth-concentration}
gives a $2p$-moment at most $C_{\beta_1,s,p}n^{-p}$.
For the conditional expectation in the last term, Jensen's inequality gives
\begin{align*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\mid A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_t\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\mid A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_t\right]}
             -\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\right]}\right|^{2p}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[\left|\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\mid A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_t\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\mid A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_t\right]}
             -\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\right]}\right|^{2p}\right]}
 \leq&~\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[|S_3(\beta,t)-\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\right]}|^{2p}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[|S_3(\beta,t)-\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}}\left[S_3(\beta,t)\right]}|^{2p}\right]}\\
 \leq&~C_{\beta_1,s,p}n^{-p},
\end{align*}
where the last inequality is again
\Cref{lem:inverse-poly-fixed-depth-concentration}.
Applying $|a+b-2c|^{2p}\leq3^{2p-1}
(|a|^{2p}+|b|^{2p}+2^{2p}|c|^{2p})$ to the decomposition proves
the moment bound, and hence the claim.
<!-- end proof-46 -->
<!-- proof-47: body_md -->
We first prove part~\textup{(i)}. We choose
\begin{equation*}
 t_0=\min\left(T,\frac{(1-\beta_1)^2(1-\beta_1^2)}{4\beta_1^2}\right),
 \qquad \kappa_0=\frac{(1-\beta_1)^2}{8}.
\end{equation*}
At $u=0$, the Hessian is
\begin{equation*}
 \nabla_u^2F_{\mathrm{TAP}}(0;A^{\mathrm{full}},y,q_\beta(t))
 =I_n-\beta A^{\mathrm{full}}+\beta^2(1-q_\beta(t))I_n.
\end{equation*}
For every $H>0$ and $\beta\in[\beta_0,\beta_1]$, we apply \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-initial-geometry} at $u=0$ and use $4\kappa_0=(1-\beta_1)^2/2$ to obtain
\begin{equation*}
 \mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}\left\{
 \left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}>4\ \text{or}\
 \exists t\in[0,t_0]:
 \lambda_{\min}\bigl(I_n-\beta A^{\mathrm{full}}+\beta^2(1-q_\beta(t))I_n\bigr)<4\kappa_0
 \right\}\le C\mathrm{e}^{-cn}\le C_Hn^{-H}
\end{equation*}
for all sufficiently large $n$.

We next prove part~\textup{(ii)}. We fix a reference pair $(\bar\beta,s)$ in $[\beta_0,\beta_1]\times[t_0,T]$ and use \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-positive-geometry,lem:inverse-poly-positive-posterior} with the same fixed AMP depth. {Their proofs use stability, \Cref{lem:gaussian-enlargement-boosting}, and overlap estimates on the compact time interval $[t_0/2,2T]\subseteq(0,\infty)$.}
We obtain an open rectangle $U_{\bar\beta,s}$ around $(\bar\beta,s)$ and constants $r,\kappa>0$ and $K\in\mathbb Z_{\geq0}$.

For every fixed $(\beta,t)\in U_{\bar\beta,s}\cap([\beta_0,\beta_1]\times[t_0,T])$, write $\widehat m=\widehat m_K(Y_t)$.
By \Cref{lem:inverse-poly-positive-geometry}, on one event of $\mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}$-probability at least $1-C\mathrm{e}^{-cn}$, the pair $(A^{\mathrm{full}},Y_t)$ satisfies $(\kappa r/16)$-small TAP residual and $(4r,4\kappa)$-local strong convexity at depth $K$, together with $\left\VertA^{\mathrm{full}}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq4$ and $\left\VertY_t\right\Vert_\infty\leq n^2$.
For every fixed integer $p\geq1$, the posterior-mean estimate at the same $K$ and $(\beta,t)$ is
\begin{equation*}
 \mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}\left\{\left\Vertm(A,Y_t)-\widehat m\right\Vert_2>r\sqrt n\right\}
 \leq C_pn^{-p}.
\end{equation*}
For each reference pair $(\bar\beta,s)$, we choose a closed rectangle contained in $U_{\bar\beta,s}$ with $(\bar\beta,s)$ strictly inside it.
By compactness, we retain finitely many such rectangles $I_1,\ldots,I_{\ell_{\max}}$, with their corresponding $K_\ell,r_\ell,\kappa_\ell$, so that every pair in $[\beta_0,\beta_1]\times[t_0,T]$ lies strictly inside at least one $I_\ell$.
We now fix $H>0$ and set $p=\max(1,\lceil H\rceil)$. For fixed $\ell$ and $(\beta,t)\in I_\ell\cap([\beta_0,\beta_1]\times[t_0,T])$, let $G_{\ell,\beta,t}$ be the event in part~\textup{(ii)}. Since $\kappa_\ell r_\ell/16\leq\kappa_\ell r_\ell/8$, by the above two probability bounds and a union bound, {for suitable constants $C_H,n_H$ and $n\geq n_H$, we have}
\begin{equation*}
 \mathbb P_{n,\beta}^{\mathrm{pl,aug}}(G_{\ell,\beta,t}^c)
 \leq C_\ell\mathrm{e}^{-c_\ell n}+C_{\ell,p}n^{-p}
 \leq C_Hn^{-H}.\qedhere
\end{equation*}
<!-- end proof-47 -->
<!-- lemma-44: body_md -->
\label{lem:strong-field-correlation}
Fix $C_1\geq0$ and $\delta\in(0,1)$.
There is a constant $C=C(C_1,\delta)>0$ such that, for every fixed $C_2\geq0$ and all sufficiently large $m$, the following holds.
If $J\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}$ is symmetric with $\left\VertJ\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_1$ and $\max_{|S|\leq\delta m}\left\Vert2J_{S,S}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq2/5$, then, for every $v\in\mathbb{R}^m$ with $\left\Vertv\right\Vert_2\leq C_2\sqrt m$ and every $\theta\geq C(1+C_2)$,
\begin{equation*}
 \left\Vert\operatorname{Cor}_{\pi_{J,\theta{\bm 1}_m+v}}(\sigma)\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq6.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-44 -->
<!-- lemma-45: body_md -->
\label{lem:pinned-glauber-mixing}
Fix $\delta\in(0,1)$, and let $n$ be sufficiently large depending only on $\delta$.
Let $J\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}$ be symmetric, let $h\in\mathbb{R}^n$, and set $\pi=\pi_{J,h}$.
Suppose that for every pinning $\sigma_{I^c}=x_{I^c}$ with $I\ne\varnothing$:
\begin{enumerate}
 \item If $|I|\geq\delta n$, the conditional distribution on $I$ has a correlation matrix of operator norm at most $6$.
 \item If $|I|<\delta n$, then $\lambda_{\max}(J_{I,I})-\lambda_{\min}(J_{I,I})\leq2/5$.
\end{enumerate}
Then, for every $f:\{\pm1\}^n\to\mathbb{R}$,
\begin{equation*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{\pi}}
    {\operatorname*{Var}\left[f\right]}
    {\operatorname*{Var}_{\pi}\left[f\right]}\leq\frac53 n^7\ensuremath{\+E_{\pi}\left(f,f\right)}.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-45 -->
<!-- lemma-46: body_md -->
\label{lem:terminal-disorder-tail}
For all sufficiently large $n$,
\begin{equation*}
 \mathbb P_{0,n}((\Omega_n^{\mathrm{end}})^c)
 \leq2\mathrm{e}^{-n/4}+2\mathrm{e}^{-n/(800\beta_1^2)}.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-46 -->
<!-- lemma-47: body_md -->
\label{lem:terminal-field-poincare}
There are constants $T_0,C,N>0$, depending only on $(\beta_0,\beta_1)$, such that, for every $n\geq N$, $A\in\Omega_n^{\mathrm{end}}$, $\beta\in[\beta_0,\beta_1]$, and $y\in\mathbb{R}^n$ with $\||y|-T_0{\bm 1}_n\|_2\leq2\sqrt{T_0n}$,
\begin{equation*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{\mu_{A,y}}}
    {\operatorname*{Var}\left[f\right]}
    {\operatorname*{Var}_{\mu_{A,y}}\left[f\right]}\leq Cn^7\ensuremath{\+E_{\mu_{A,y}}\left(f,f\right)}
\end{equation*}
for every $f:\{\pm1\}^n\to\mathbb{R}$, where $\mu_{A,y}$ is the Gibbs distribution at inverse temperature $\beta$ and external field $y$.
<!-- end lemma-47 -->
<!-- proof-48: body_md -->
We first choose $T_0$ large enough that
\begin{equation*}
 T_0\geq C(3\beta_1,\delta)\bigl(1+\delta^{-1/2}(2\sqrt{T_0}+3\beta_1)\bigr),
\end{equation*}
where $C(3\beta_1,\delta)$ is the constant in \Cref{lem:strong-field-correlation}.
Such a fixed $T_0$ exists because the right side grows as $O(\sqrt{T_0})$.
We now fix $A\in\Omega_n^{\mathrm{end}}$, $\beta\in[\beta_0,\beta_1]$, and $y$ with $\||y|-T_0{\bm 1}_n\|_2\leq2\sqrt{T_0n}$.

To make the external field nonnegative, we set $s_i\triangleq\operatorname{sgn}(y_i)$, with $\operatorname{sgn}(0)=1$, and $S\triangleq\operatorname{diag}(s_1,\ldots,s_n)$.
For $\widetilde x\triangleq Sx$ and $\widetilde A\triangleq SAS$, we have $S^2=I_n$ and
$\frac\beta2\langle x,Ax\rangle+\langle y,x\rangle
 =\frac\beta2\langle \widetilde x,\widetilde A\widetilde x\rangle
   +\langle |y|,\widetilde x\rangle$.

Thus, if $X\sim\mu_{A,y}$, then $\widetilde X\triangleq SX\sim\mu_{\widetilde A,|y|}$.
Since $S$ is orthogonal, $\left\Vert\widetilde A\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}=\left\VertA\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}$.
For every $I\subseteq[n]$, writing $S_I$ for the restriction of $S$ to $I$, we also have $\left\Vert\widetilde A_{I,I}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}
 =\left\VertS_I A_{I,I}S_I\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}
 =\left\VertA_{I,I}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}$.
Hence $\widetilde A\in\Omega_n^{\mathrm{end}}$.
For any $f$, we set $\widetilde f(u)\triangleq f(Su)$, so that $\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mu_{A,y}}}
    {\operatorname*{Var}\left[f\right]}
    {\operatorname*{Var}_{\mu_{A,y}}\left[f\right]}=\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mu_{\widetilde A,|y|}}}
    {\operatorname*{Var}\left[\widetilde f\right]}
    {\operatorname*{Var}_{\mu_{\widetilde A,|y|}}\left[\widetilde f\right]}$.

The change of variables only exchanges the two spin values in each coordinate, so the one-coordinate conditional variances, and hence their average, satisfy
$\ensuremath{\+E_{\mu_{A,y}}\left(f,f\right)}
 =\ensuremath{\+E_{\mu_{\widetilde A,|y|}}\left(\widetilde f,\widetilde f\right)}$.
It therefore suffices to prove the Poincar\'e inequality for $\mu_{\widetilde A,|y|}$.

We now check the two pinning conditions in \Cref{lem:pinned-glauber-mixing}.
For a nonempty $I\subseteq[n]$, we fix the spins outside $I$ to $\tau\in\{\pm1\}^{I^c}$.
Expanding the quadratic term in the Gibbs weight, we obtain, for $u\in\{\pm1\}^I$,
\begin{equation*}
 \mu_{\widetilde A,|y|}(\widetilde X_I=u\mid\widetilde X_{I^c}=\tau)
 \propto\exp\left(\frac\beta2\langle u,\widetilde A_{I,I}u\rangle
  +\langle |y|_I+\beta\widetilde A_{I,I^c}\tau,u\rangle\right),
\end{equation*}
where terms independent of $u$ are absorbed into the normalizing constant.
Thus the interaction on $I$ is $\beta\widetilde A_{I,I}$ and its field is $T_0{\bm 1}_I+v_I$, where
$v_I\triangleq |y|_I-T_0{\bm 1}_I+\beta\widetilde A_{I,I^c}\tau$.

If $|I|\geq\delta n$, we use $\left\Vert\widetilde A\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq3$ and $\left\Vert\tau\right\Vert_2\leq\sqrt n$ to obtain
\begin{align*}
 \left\Vertv_I\right\Vert_2
 \leq \||y|-T_0{\bm 1}_n\|_2
       +\beta\left\Vert\widetilde A_{I,I^c}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}\left\Vert\tau\right\Vert_2
 \leq (2\sqrt{T_0}+3\beta_1)\sqrt n
 \leq\delta^{-1/2}(2\sqrt{T_0}+3\beta_1)\sqrt{|I|}.
\end{align*}
Also, $\left\Vert\beta\widetilde A_{I,I}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq3\beta_1$, and for every $J\subseteq I$ with $|J|\leq\delta|I|$, we have $\left\Vert2\beta\widetilde A_{J,J}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq2/5$.
By \Cref{lem:strong-field-correlation} with $m=|I|$, $C_1=3\beta_1$, $C_2=\delta^{-1/2}(2\sqrt{T_0}+3\beta_1)$, and $\theta=T_0$, the correlation matrix of this pinned distribution has operator norm at most $6$ for all sufficiently large $n$.
If $|I|<\delta n$, we instead use $\widetilde A\in\Omega_n^{\mathrm{end}}$ to obtain
\begin{equation*}
 \lambda_{\max}(\beta\widetilde A_{I,I})-\lambda_{\min}(\beta\widetilde A_{I,I})
 \leq2\left\Vert\beta\widetilde A_{I,I}\right\Vert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac25.
\end{equation*}
We have verified both hypotheses of \Cref{lem:pinned-glauber-mixing}, so we can apply it to $\mu_{\widetilde A,|y|}$ and $\widetilde f$.
Using the variance and Dirichlet-form identities above, we obtain
\begin{equation*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{\mu_{A,y}}}
    {\operatorname*{Var}\left[f\right]}
    {\operatorname*{Var}_{\mu_{A,y}}\left[f\right]}
 =\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mu_{\widetilde A,|y|}}}
    {\operatorname*{Var}\left[\widetilde f\right]}
    {\operatorname*{Var}_{\mu_{\widetilde A,|y|}}\left[\widetilde f\right]}
 \leq Cn^7\ensuremath{\+E_{\mu_{\widetilde A,|y|}}\left(\widetilde f,\widetilde f\right)}
 =Cn^7\ensuremath{\+E_{\mu_{A,y}}\left(f,f\right)}.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end proof-48 -->
<!-- proof-49: body_md -->
We take $T_0,C,N$ from \Cref{lem:terminal-field-poincare}.
By \Cref{lem:terminal-disorder-tail}, the event $\Omega_n^{\mathrm{end}}$ has $\mathbb P_{0,n}$-probability at least $1-C\exp(-cn)$, after adjusting $C,c$.
We fix $A\in\Omega_n^{\mathrm{end}}$ and $\beta\in[\beta_0,\beta_1]$, and let $X,B,Y$ be as in \Cref{lem:app-wpi-two-path-terminal}.
We split the expectation according to whether the terminal field satisfies the condition in \Cref{lem:terminal-field-poincare}.
For this, we define the event
\begin{equation*}
 \mathcal G\triangleq\left\{\||Y_{T_0}|-T_0{\bm 1}_n\|_2\leq2\sqrt{T_0n}\right\}.
\end{equation*}
We first bound the probability of $\mathcal G^c$.
We write $Y_{T_0}=T_0X+\sqrt{T_0}G$, where $G\sim N(0,I_n)$ is independent of $X$, and use the coordinatewise inequality $\bigl||Y_{T_0,i}|-T_0\bigr|\leq\sqrt{T_0}|G_i|$.
By Markov's inequality and the Gaussian moment-generating function, we obtain
\begin{align*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[\mathcal G^c\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}\left[\mathcal G^c\right]}
 \leq&~\ifthenelse{\isempty{G\sim N(0,I_n)}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[\|G\|_2>2\sqrt n\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{G\sim N(0,I_n)}\left[\|G\|_2>2\sqrt n\right]}\\
 \leq&~\exp(-3n/2)\ifthenelse{\isempty{G\sim N(0,I_n)}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\exp(3\|G\|_2^2/8)\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{G\sim N(0,I_n)}\left[\exp(3\|G\|_2^2/8)\right]}\\
 =&~\exp(-(3/2-\log2)n).
\end{align*}
By the law of total variance, we have that
\begin{align*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\ensuremath{\+E_{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}\left(f,f\right)}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}\left[\ensuremath{\+E_{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}\left(f,f\right)}\right]}
 =&~\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\ifthenelse{\isempty{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{Var}\left[f(X)\mid X_{-i},Y_{T_0}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{Var}_{}\left[f(X)\mid X_{-i},Y_{T_0}\right]}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}\left[\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{Var}\left[f(X)\mid X_{-i},Y_{T_0}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{Var}_{}\left[f(X)\mid X_{-i},Y_{T_0}\right]}\right]}\\
 \leq&~\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\ifthenelse{\isempty{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A}}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{Var}\left[f(X)\mid X_{-i}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{Var}_{}\left[f(X)\mid X_{-i}\right]}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A}}\left[\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}
    {\operatorname*{Var}\left[f(X)\mid X_{-i}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{Var}_{}\left[f(X)\mid X_{-i}\right]}\right]}
 =\ensuremath{\+E_{\mu_{\beta,A}}\left(f,f\right)}.
\end{align*}
On $\mathcal G$, we apply \Cref{lem:terminal-field-poincare} to bound the posterior variance by $Cn^7\ensuremath{\+E_{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}\left(f,f\right)}$.
On $\mathcal G^c$, we use $\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}}
    {\operatorname*{Var}\left[f\right]}
    {\operatorname*{Var}_{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}\left[f\right]}\leq\operatorname{osc}(f)^2/4$, which holds because $f$ takes values in $[\inf f,\sup f]$.
Splitting the expectation over these two events, we obtain
\begin{align*}
 \ifthenelse{\isempty{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}}
    {\operatorname*{Var}\left[f\right]}
    {\operatorname*{Var}_{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}\left[f\right]}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}\left[\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}}
    {\operatorname*{Var}\left[f\right]}
    {\operatorname*{Var}_{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}\left[f\right]}\right]}
 =&~\ifthenelse{\isempty{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\1_{\mathcal G}\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}}
    {\operatorname*{Var}\left[f\right]}
    {\operatorname*{Var}_{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}\left[f\right]}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}\left[\1_{\mathcal G}\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}}
    {\operatorname*{Var}\left[f\right]}
    {\operatorname*{Var}_{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}\left[f\right]}\right]}
   +\ifthenelse{\isempty{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\1_{\mathcal G^c}\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}}
    {\operatorname*{Var}\left[f\right]}
    {\operatorname*{Var}_{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}\left[f\right]}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}\left[\1_{\mathcal G^c}\ifthenelse{\isempty{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}}
    {\operatorname*{Var}\left[f\right]}
    {\operatorname*{Var}_{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}\left[f\right]}\right]}\\
 \leq&~Cn^7\ifthenelse{\isempty{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\1_{\mathcal G}\ensuremath{\+E_{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}\left(f,f\right)}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}\left[\1_{\mathcal G}\ensuremath{\+E_{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}\left(f,f\right)}\right]}
   +\frac{\operatorname{osc}(f)^2}{4}\ifthenelse{\isempty{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[\mathcal G^c\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}\left[\mathcal G^c\right]}\\
 \leq&~Cn^7\ifthenelse{\isempty{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\ensuremath{\+E_{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}\left(f,f\right)}\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}\left[\ensuremath{\+E_{\mu_{A,Y_{T_0}}}\left(f,f\right)}\right]}
   +\tfrac14\exp(-cn)\operatorname{osc}(f)^2\\
 \leq&~Cn^7\ensuremath{\+E_{\mu_{\beta,A}}\left(f,f\right)}
   +\tfrac14\exp(-cn)\operatorname{osc}(f)^2,
\end{align*}
where we use the bound on $\ifthenelse{\isempty{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}\left[\mathcal G^c\right]}
    {\operatorname*{\mathbb{P}}_{X\sim\mu_{\beta,A},\,B}\left[\mathcal G^c\right]}$ and the averaged Dirichlet-form inequality proved above, and decrease $c$ if necessary.
<!-- end proof-49 -->
